COMPLETE\n   579→\n   580→### Check 1 — Accuracy vs chance\n   581→| Variable | Val. balanced acc | Uniform chance | Ratio | Verdict |\n   582→|---|---|---|---|---|\n   583→| reward_zone_distance | 0.6200 | 0.1429 | 4.34× | well above chance |\n   584→| position | 0.7657 | 0.2000 | 3.83× | well above chance |\n   585→| speed | 0.6258 | 0.2000 | 3.13× | well above chance |\n   586→| lick | 0.7658 | 0.5000 | 1.53× | above the 1.5× guideline |\n   587→| reward_zone_location | 0.8739 | 0.3333 | 2.62× | well above chance |\n   588→| reward_outcome | 0.6027 | 0.5000 | 1.21× | below 1.5× — investigated below |\n   589→\n   590→No output is at or below chance.\n   591→\n   592→**`reward_outcome` investigation.** `reward_outcome` is a *per-trial* label, but the\n   593→decoder predicts it at *every timepoint*. Before the animal reaches the reward zone\n   594→there is no information anywhere in the brain about whether this trial's reward will\n   595→be delivered or omitted (omission is decided by a random number generator and is only\n   596→revealed at the zone), so roughly the first half of every trial is unpredictable by\n   597→construction and drags the whole-trial balanced accuracy toward 0.5.\n   598→\n   599→I tested this directly (`cache/diag_reward.py`): for 7 sessions spread across the\n   600→dataset, an independent PCA(100) + balanced logistic regression trained on the first\n   601→75 % of trials and tested on the last 25 % gives\n   602→\n   603→| timepoints used | mean validation balanced accuracy |\n   604→|---|---|\n   605→| all in-trial timepoints | **0.600** |\n   606→| only timepoints past the end of the reward zone | **0.675** |\n   607→\n   608→The 0.600 from this independent model matches the decoder's 0.6027 almost exactly,\n   609→and restricting to the informative part of the trial raises it by 0.075. So the low\n   610→ratio reflects the structure of the task, not a conversion bug. I deliberately did\n   611→**not** \"fix\" it by, e.g., labelling pre-zone timepoints differently — the Decoder\n   612→Task specifies reward outcome as a per-trial output.\n   613→\n   614→**`lick` (1.53×)**: licking is decoded from CA1 dF/F at 0.766 balanced accuracy, i.e.\n   615→the decoder recovers the lick bouts (clearly visible in `predictions.png`). The\n   616→modest ratio is simply because a binary variable has chance 0.5.\n   617→\n   618→### Check 2 — Accuracy comparison to the paper\n   619→I searched the paper and `methods.txt` for every reported decoding result. The **only**\n   620→decoder in the paper is the circular–linear position decoder of Fig. 3\n   621→(`reward_relative/decode.py :: CircularRegression`), and it is reported as a\n   622→**\"decode score\" = mean cos(y − ŷ)** (1 = perfect, 0 = chance) and as a z-score\n   623→relative to a circular-shift shuffle — not as a classification accuracy, and only for\n   624→*reward-relative position* restricted to RR / TR / non-RR **place-cell subpopulations**\n   625→within single sessions.\n   626→\n   627→| Variable | My validation balanced accuracy | Paper's reported accuracy |\n   628→|---|---|---|\n   629→| reward_zone_distance | 0.6200 | no comparable number (Fig. 3 reports a cosine \"decode score\" for a circular RR-position variable, over selected place-cell subsets) |\n   630→| position | 0.7657 | not decoded in the paper |\n   631→| speed | 0.6258 | not decoded in the paper |\n   632→| lick | 0.7658 | not decoded in the paper |\n   633→| reward_zone_location | 0.8739 | not decoded in the paper |\n   634→| reward_outcome | 0.6027 | not decoded in the paper |\n   635→\n   636→There is therefore no paper number that my accuracies can be lower than. The\n   637→qualitative claim that the paper *does* make — that CA1 population activity carries a\n   638→strong code for position relative to reward — is reproduced: reward-zone distance is\n   639→decoded at 4.3× chance and reward-zone identity at 2.6× chance.\n   640→\n   641→### Check 3 — Train vs validation gap\n   642→| Output | Train | Val | Train/Val |\n   643→|---|---|---|---|\n   644→| reward_zone_distance | 0.7901 | 0.6200 | 1.27 |\n   645→| position | 0.8900 | 0.7657 | 1.16 |\n   646→| speed | 0.7226 | 0.6258 | 1.15 |\n   647→| lick | 0.7926 | 0.7658 | 1.03 |\n   648→| reward_zone_location | 0.9639 | 0.8739 | 1.10 |\n   649→| reward_outcome | 0.9379 | 0.6027 | **1.56** |\n   650→\n   651→Only `reward_outcome` exceeds the 1.5× guideline. Cause: it is constant within a trial\n   652→and the model has 152 session-specific 100 × n_neurons projection matrices trained for\n   653→200 epochs, so it can memorise which *training* trials were rewarded from slow,\n   654→trial-specific fluctuations in the population state. This is over-fitting of the\n   655→reference decoder on a per-trial label, not data leakage from the conversion:\n   656→- The train/validation split is over **whole trials** (`train_validate_decoder` splits\n   657→  trial indices), so no timepoint of a validation trial is ever seen in training.\n   658→- None of the four decoder **inputs** reveals the current trial's outcome:\n   659→  `previous_trial_reward` is the *previous* trial (verified against the raw NWB for all\n   660→  11,945 consecutive pairs), and `time`, `environment`, `trial_number` are outcome-free.\n   661→- I confirmed with the independent per-session model in Check 1 that ~0.60 is the\n   662→  honest generalisation level for this variable.\n   663→- The same pattern appears for `reward_zone_location` to a much smaller degree\n   664→  (1.10), which is expected because that label is genuinely encoded in the population.\n   665→\n   666→### Other debugging steps run\n   667→1. Output values verified against the raw NWB on specific trials of 4 sessions\n   668→   (Step 10, Check 2) — exact matches.\n   669→2. Its output is what the NWB `BehavioralTimeSeries` contains (all behaviour channels share the imaging timestamps). |\n| `dff` | `preprocessing.py` | PROCESSING | dF/F for one channel. Restricts to within-trial samples (`keep_teleports=False`), neuropil subtraction `F − 0.7·Fneu` then adds back the per-trial mean neuropil, **maximin baseline** (Gaussian σ=15 samples → `minimum_filter1d(300)` → `maximum_filter1d(300)`), `dF/F=(F−F0)/|F0|`, then 2-sample σ Gaussian smoothing per trial; optional OASIS deconvolution (`suite2p.extraction.dcnv.oasis(dff, 2000, tau, fs)`). |\n| `default_dff_method` | `utilities.py` | PROCESSING | `neuropil_method='subtract'`, `baseline_method='maximin'`, `neu_coef=0.7`, `keep_teleports=False`. |\n| `multi_anim_sess` | `utilities.py` | PROCESSING/CURATION | Top-level per-day loader: calls `pp.dff(...)`, `behav.get_trial_types`, `behav.get_reward_zones`, `behav.define_trial_subsets`, `calc_place_cells`. |\n| `get_trial_types` | `behavior.py` | PROCESSING | Per-trial `isreward` = `any(reward>0) and any(rzone>0)` within `[trial_start_inds[i], teleport_inds[i])`; per-trial `morph` (0 = ENV1, 1 = ENV2). |\n| `get_reward_zones` | `behavior.py` | PROCESSING | Per-trial reward-zone coordinates/labels from the **scene name**; on switch scenes the zone changes at `change_trial = 30`. Zone coords come from `reward_zone_dict`: A→`X`=[80,130], B→`Y`=[200,250], C→`Z`=[320,370]. |\n| `reward_zone_dict`, `map_labels`, `env_morph_dict` | `behavior.py` | REFERENCE | Zone coordinates; `Env1→0`, `Env2→1`. |\n| `define_trial_subsets` | `behavior.py` | CURATION | Splits trials into pre-/post-switch sets (not needed for the decoder, but confirms the switch-at-30 convention). |\n| `correct_lick_sensor_error` | `behavior.py` | CURATION | Lick-sensor failure detection (see Step 3). |\n| `nansmooth` | `utilities.py` | PROCESSING | NaN-tolerant Gaussian smoothing used by `dff`. |\n| `decode.py :: CircularRegression` | `decode.py` | ANALYSIS | The paper's own decoder (circular–linear regression on **deconvolved** activity). Not the decoder used here, but tells us which neural signal the authors decoded from. |\n\n### Notes\n- Imaging data: 2-photon **calcium imaging**, so ΔF/F **must be computed** (the NWB\n  stores raw suite2p `F`, `Fneu` and suite2p's own `Deconvolved`/`spks`, none of which\n  are ΔF/F).\n- Cell quality filtering: suite2p `iscell` (manual curation) + speed-correlation\n  interneuron exclusion (Step 3).\n- The reference `dff()` slices trials as `[start-1 : stop-1]` while all behavioural\n  code (`get_trial_types`, etc.) slices `[start : stop]`. This 1-frame inconsistency\n  inside the reference is resolved here by using `[start : stop]` **everywhere**, so\n  that the neural and behavioural streams are exactly aligned (see Step 5, decisions).\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Data Structure\n- `/app/data/sub-m<N>/sub-m<N>_ses-<DD>_behavior+ophys.nwb`; `ses-<DD>` is the\n  1-indexed **experiment day** (01–14). 11 subjects × 14 days, except `m11` which\n  starts at day 03 (12 sessions) → **152 sessions**, matching `dandiset.yaml`\n  (`numberOfFiles: 152`, `numberOfSubjects: 11`).\n- `nwb.identifier` = `/data/InVivoDA/GCAMP<N>/<dd_mm_yyyy>/<scene>` → gives the\n  **scene name** (e.g. `Env1_LocationB_to_A`), exactly the string the reference\n  `get_reward_zones` / `define_trial_subsets` parse. Verified against\n  `code/src/reward_relative/sessions_dict.py`: `GCAMP<N>` ↔ `sub-m<N>`, and every\n  scene/date pair matches.\n- `nwb.processing['ophys']`:\n  - `Fluorescence/plane0` — `(n_frames, n_roi)` float32, suite2p `F`\n  - `Neuropil/plane0` — suite2p `Fneu`\n  - `Deconvolved/plane0` — suite2p `spks` computed from **raw F** (values in raw\n    fluorescence units, e.g. 0–3800), *not* the paper's deconvolution of dF/F\n  - `ImageSegmentation/PlaneSegmentation` — `pixel_mask`, **`iscell` (n_roi, 2)**,\n    `planeIdx`\n- `nwb.processing['behavior']/BehavioralTimeSeries` — all sampled on the imaging\n  frame clock (shared `timestamps`), `dt = 0.064484 s` (15.5078 Hz) **identical in\n  all 152 files**:\n  `position` (cm), `speed` (cm/s), `lick` (cumulative count per frame),\n  `reward_zone` (count of reward-zone-entry samples per frame), `environment`\n  (−1 ITI, 0 ENV1, 1 ENV2), `trial number`, `trial_start` (binary), `teleport`\n  (binary), `scanning` (−1/1), `autoreward` (all zeros in every file — unusable),\n  and `Reward` (a separate TimeSeries with one timestamp per reward delivery,\n  0.004 mL).\n- `nwb.acquisition['TwoPhotonSeries']` is a `(1,1,1)` placeholder (no movie).\n- No `trials`/`units`/`epochs` tables.\n\n### Dataset Size (from data files)\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Sessions | 152 (11 subjects; 14 each except m11 = 12) |\n| ROIs (total, before `iscell`) | 260,091 |\n| Neurons (`iscell==1`, total) | 138,678 |\n| Neurons / session | mean 912.4, **min 155, max 2341** |\n| Subjects | 11 (m3, m4, m7, m11–m15, m17–m19) |\n| Sessions / subject | 14 (m11: 12) |\n| Trials (total, `trial_start` events) | 12,216 |\n| Trials / session | mean 80.4, min 41, max 100 |\n| Imaging rate | 15.5078125 Hz per plane in all sessions (m17, m18 are 2-plane, 31.0156 Hz volume rate → 15.5078 Hz/plane) |\n| Session duration | ~1,200–1,800 s |\n| Frames inside trials | ~72–78% of all frames (the rest is the ITI/teleport period) |\n\n---\n\n## Step 3: Reference Text Reading\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Expected Statistics (from paper/methods)\n| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n|-----------|-------|--------------|\n| Subjects (switch task) | 11 | \"Each mouse encountered a different starting reward zone and sequence of reward zone switches, counterbalanced across mice (n = 11 mice).\" |\n| Mice starting in ENV 2 | 2 (m17, m18) | \"Most mice began the task in ENV 1 (n = 9 mice; two mice (m17 and m18) began in ENV 2).\" |\n| Sessions / subject | 14 (m11 starts day 3) | \"for a total of 14 days\"; \"The task was imaged starting from day 1 for all mice except m11, for whom imaging started on day 3\" |\n| Neurons / session | **155–2172** | \"This approach yielded 155–2172 putative pyramidal neurons per session\" |\n| Trials / session | 80.5 ± 7.4 | \"mean ± s.d., 80.5 ± 7.4 trials across 14 mice, all imaging days\"; \"We targeted 80–100 trials per session\" |\n| Trials (total, 11 switch mice) | 12,376 | \"n = 81 out of 12,376 trials removed across 11 switch mice\" |\n| Lick-sensor error trials | 81 (~0.65%) | \"A very small number of trials with erroneous lick detection ... (~0.65% of all imaged trials, n = 81 out of 12,376 trials)\" |\n| Reward omission rate | ~15% | \"the reward was randomly omitted on ~15% of trials\" |\n| Neural data time bin | 0.0645 s (15.5 Hz) | \"the 0.0645 s imaging frame samples\"; \"collected ... at ~15.5 Hz\" |\n| Behaviour time bin | same as neural | VR data is interpolated onto the 2P frame clock (`vr_align_to_2P`) |\n| Interneurons excluded by speed correlation | 0.42 ± 0.85% of cells | \"excluding 0.42 ± 0.85% of cells (mean ± s.d. across mice and days)\" |\n| Track length | 450 cm | \"a unidirectional 450 cm virtual linear track\" |\n| Reward zones | A 80–130, B 200–250, C 320–370 cm | \"zone A, 80–130 cm; zone B, 200–250 cm; zone C, 320–370 cm\" |\n| Switch trial | after 30 trials | \"Each switch occurred after 30 trials.\" |\n| dF/F baseline | maximin, 20 s window, within trial | \"baseline fluorescence was calculated within each trial independently using a maximin procedure with a 20 s sliding window\" |\n| dF/F smoothing | 2-sample (~0.129 s) Gaussian | \"then smoothed with a two-sample (~0.129 s) s.d. Gaussian kernel\" |\n| Speed threshold used for *spatial* analyses | 2 cm/s | \"we excluded activity when the animal was moving at <2 cm s−1\" |\n\n### Processing Details\n- **Temporal alignment**: VR behaviour is interpolated onto the 2P frame times\n  (`vr_align_to_2P`); the NWB already stores it that way (one shared timestamp\n  vector). Trials run from the `trial_start` event to the `teleport` event.\n- **Temporal binning**: native imaging frame, 64.484 ms, identical in all sessions.\n- **dF/F**: see Step 1 (`dff()`), matching the methods text exactly\n  (300 samples / 15.5 Hz = 19.3 s ≈ the \"20 s sliding window\").\n- **Deconvolution**: OASIS on the smoothed dF/F; the paper notes this \"is not\n  interpreted as a spike rate but rather as a method to eliminate the asymmetric\n  smoothing of the calcium signal\".\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules**:\n1. suite2p manual curation → `iscell[:,0] == 1` (\"Manual curation eliminated ROIs\n   containing multiple somata or dendrites, lacking visually obvious transients,\n   suspected of overexpressing the calcium indicator or exhibiting high and\n   continuous fluorescence fluctuation typical of putative interneurons\").\n2. Pearson `corr(dF/F, speed) > 0.5` → putative interneuron, excluded.\n3. (Place-cell selection is used for *some* figure analyses; not applied here —\n   see Step 5 decisions.)\n\n**Trial curation rules**:\n1. Trials = `[trial_start, teleport)`.\n2. Lick-sensor failure trials removed: \">30% of the 0.0645 s imaging frame samples\n   in the trial containing a cumulative lick count >2\".\n3. (Added here) trials overlapping frames with `scanning < 0` (no 2P data) and\n   trials shorter than 2 frames.\n\n### Decoders Trained (in the paper)\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n|---|---|\n| Reward-relative (circular) position, from deconvolved activity of RR / TR / non-RR place cells | Reported as a \"decode score\" = mean `cos(y − ŷ)` (1 = perfect, 0 = chance), not a classification accuracy. Fig. 3b/3c report scores and z-scores vs. a circular-shift shuffle. No % accuracies are reported anywhere in the paper, so there is no directly comparable number for the outputs decoded here. |\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Paper says | Resolution |\n|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n| Total trials | – | 12,216 `trial_start` events over 152 imaged sessions | 12,376 trials over 11 switch mice | Difference = 160 = exactly 2 sessions × 80 trials. m11 days 1–2 have VR behaviour but **no imaging** (\"imaging started on day 3 [for m11]\"), so they are absent from DANDI but were counted in the behavioural total. Consistent. |\n| Lick-error trials | `correct_lick_sensor_error` (a different, position-based helper) | My implementation of the methods rule finds **exactly 81** trials | 81 trials (~0.65%) | Exact match → the trial-curation rule is implemented correctly. |\n| Neurons per session | – | 155 – 2341 (`iscell==1`) | 155 – 2172 | Lower bound matches **exactly**. Upper bound: only 3 sessions exceed 2172 (m18 days 1, 3, 8: 2281/2341/2314). m18 is a 2-plane animal; the paper's upper bound is plausibly quoted per-plane or from an earlier curation round. Speed-correlation exclusion removes only ~0.4%, so it cannot account for the gap. Difference is 3/152 sessions and <8%; `iscell` filtering is clearly the right criterion. |\n| Reward omission | `isreward = any(reward>0) & any(rzone>0)` | 15.34% of trials un-rewarded (1,874/12,216) | \"~15% of trials\" omitted | Match. |\n| Switch trial | `change_trial = 30` default | For every one of the 77 switch sessions, the last trial with an observed pre-switch zone entry is ≤ 29 and the first with a post-switch entry is ≥ 30 | \"Each switch occurred after 30 trials\" | Consistent; `change_trial = 30` used. |\n| Reward-zone coordinates | `reward_zone_dict` X/Y/Z = [80,130]/[200,250]/[320,370], `map_labels` A→X, B→Y, C→Z | Reward-zone entry always occurs at 80 / 200 / 320 cm (±3 cm) | A 80–130, B 200–250, C 320–370 | Match. |\n| `autoreward` channel | used by the VR task | **all zeros** in every one of the 152 files | first 10 trials of a new condition get automatic reward | The channel was not populated in the NWB export. Not needed for any decoder input/output; automatically-delivered rewards still appear in the `Reward` series, so `reward_outcome` is unaffected. |\n| Environment coding | `env_morph_dict`: Env1→0, Env2→1 | `environment` channel is −1 (ITI), 0, 1 | ENV1 / ENV2 | Match. Each trial has a single environment value (verified for all 12,216 trials). |\n| Neural signal for decoding | paper's own decoder uses **deconvolved** activity | NWB `Deconvolved` is suite2p's spks from *raw* F, not the paper's OASIS-on-dF/F | – | Recomputed both from `F`/`Fneu` with the reference `dff()`; dF/F chosen as the decoder input (see Step 5, decision 2). |\n\n---\n\n## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Variable Mapping\n| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n| `ophys/Fluorescence/plane0`, `ophys/Neuropil/plane0`, `ImageSegmentation.iscell` | `neural` | `iscell` filter → `dff()` (neuropil subtract, maximin baseline, 2-sample Gaussian) → speed-correlation interneuron exclusion → slice `[trial_start, teleport)` | `preprocessing.dff`, `utilities.default_dff_method`, `utilities.nansmooth` | float32, `(n_neurons, T)` per trial |\n| `behavior/position` timestamps | `input[0]` `time_from_trial_start_s` | `t − t[trial_start]` | – | seconds, time-varying |\n| `behavior/environment` | `input[1]` `environment` | unique value within the trial (0 = ENV1, 1 = ENV2) | `behavior.get_trial_types` (`morph`), `env_morph_dict` | constant within trial |\n| trial index | `input[2]` `trial_number` | 0-indexed position of the `trial_start` event in the session | – | constant within trial |\n| `behavior/Reward` + `behavior/reward_zone` | `input[3]` `previous_trial_reward` | `isreward` of trial *i−1*; trial 0 → 1 | `behavior.get_trial_types` | constant within trial |\n| `behavior/position` + scene-derived reward zone | `output[0]` `reward_zone_distance` | signed distance to nearest point of the zone → 7 bins | `behavior.get_reward_zones`, `reward_zone_dict` | time-varying |\n| `behavior/position` | `output[1]` `position` | `digitize` at 90/180/270/360 cm | – | time-varying |\n| `behavior/speed` | `output[2]` `speed` | `digitize` at 2/10/20/40 cm/s | – | time-varying |\n| `behavior/lick` | `output[3]` `lick` | `count > 0` | paper: \"lick counts were converted to a binary vector\" | time-varying |\n| `nwb.identifier` scene + `change_trial=30` | `output[4]` `reward_zone_location` | A=0, B=1, C=2 | `behavior.get_reward_zones` | constant within trial |\n| `behavior/Reward`, `behavior/reward_zone` | `output[5]` `reward_outcome` | `any(reward) and any(rzone)` | `behavior.get_trial_types` | constant within trial |\n| `nwb.subject.subject_id` | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | – | – | 11 mice |\n| – | `brain_regions` = `['CA1']`, `brain_region_idx` = zeros | – | – | all imaging is dorsal CA1 |\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Time bin = native imaging frame (64.484 ms).** Every one of the 152 sessions has\n   exactly the same `dt` (15.5078 Hz per plane), and the NWB behaviour is already\n   interpolated onto that clock by the reference `vr_align_to_2P`. Using the native\n   grid therefore satisfies \"same bin size for all trials and sessions\" with **zero\n   resampling**, i.e. no risk of introducing temporal misalignment between the\n   neural and behavioural streams.\n2. **Neural signal = dF/F** (computed exactly as in `preprocessing.dff`), not the\n   deconvolved trace. Rationale: (a) the NWB's `Deconvolved` array is suite2p's spks\n   from *raw* F and does **not** correspond to the paper's deconvolution, so it had\n   to be recomputed either way; (b) the paper itself uses binned dF/F for its spatial\n   peak identification \"as this signal is the closest to the raw data\"; (c) I tested\n   both signals with a PCA+logistic position decoder on three sessions — dF/F was\n   equal or better in every case (m11 d3 0.53 vs 0.45, m19 d6 0.77 vs 0.63, m13 d8\n   0.40 vs 0.41), which is expected because the decoder reads instantaneous activity\n   and dF/F retains the temporal integration that a sparse event train discards.\n   The deconvolution step itself is implemented and validated but not used.\n3. **No speed threshold on the neural data.** The paper excludes samples with speed\n   <2 cm/s from *spatial* analyses. Here speed is a decoder **output** whose class 0\n   is exactly \"< 2 cm/s\", so dropping those samples would make that class impossible\n   to decode and would also punch holes in the time-varying outputs. Required\n   deviation, documented.\n4. **No place-cell selection.** Place-cell identification in the paper depends on a\n   stochastic 100× shuffle and is used to *restrict* specific figure analyses; the\n   decoder should see the full curated pyramidal population. All `iscell`,\n   non-interneuron cells are kept.\n5. **Trial window `[trial_start, teleport)` for both neural and behaviour.** The\n   reference `dff()` uses `[start-1, stop-1)` while the reference behaviour code uses\n   `[start, stop)`; using `[start, stop)` for both keeps the streams exactly aligned.\n   The effect on the dF/F baseline is one frame at each end of a ≥113-frame trial,\n   i.e. negligible for a 300-sample maximin window.\n6. **Reward zone from the scene name + `change_trial = 30`** (the reference method),\n   *cross-checked* against the position at which the `reward_zone` channel fires on\n   every trial where it fires. The data-driven check cannot be used alone because the\n   channel does not fire on reward-omission trials (~15%).\n7. **`previous_trial_reward` for trial 0 = 1 (rewarded).** Each imaging session is\n   immediately preceded by ~30 warm-up trials of the same task with the same reward\n   zone (\"Before the imaging session, mice were provided 30 'warm-up' trials using\n   the task and reward zone from the previous day\"), and rewards are omitted on only\n   ~15% of trials, so \"rewarded\" is the correct prior for the trial preceding trial 0.\n8. **Trial exclusions**: the 81 lick-sensor-failure trials (the paper NaNs their lick\n   data; since `lick` is a decoder output and NaNs are not allowed, the trials are\n   dropped), trials overlapping `scanning < 0`, trials < 2 frames.\n9. **Per-trial variables are broadcast to `(d, T)`** rather than stored as 1-D, so\n   that all inputs/outputs share one array per trial and the time-varying and\n   per-trial variables can coexist in a single matrix.\n10. **`brain_regions = ['CA1']`.** All recordings are dorsal hippocampal CA1; the two\n    planes in m17/m18 are deep/superficial CA1 and are \"pooled for all analyses\n    except those in Extended Data Fig. 7\".\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [x] Total `trial_start` events == 12,216 and reconciles with the paper's 12,376.\n- [x] Lick-error trial detection reproduces the paper's **81** trials.\n- [x] `iscell` neuron counts: min == 155 (paper's lower bound).\n- [x] Reward omission rate ≈ 15%.\n- [x] Reward-zone switch consistent with trial 30 in all 77 switch sessions.\n- [x] Scene-derived reward zone agrees with the observed `reward_zone` entry position\n      on every trial where the zone fires (checked per session during conversion).\n- [x] Speed-correlation interneuron exclusion ≈ 0.4% of cells.\n- [ ] Spot-check neural / input / output values against the raw NWB (Step 10).\n- [ ] Position within a trial spans ~0 → 450 cm; speed, lick ranges sensible.\n- [ ] Decoder accuracy above chance for every output.\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`/app/convert_data.py`. Structure:\n- `nansmooth` — verbatim port of the reference NaN-tolerant Gaussian smoother.\n- `compute_dff` — port of `reward_relative/preprocessing.py::dff` for the\n  single-channel, `neuropil_method='subtract'`, `baseline_method='maximin'`,\n  `keep_teleports=False` configuration that `multi_anim_sess` uses for these data.\n- `reward_zone_labels_from_scene` — port of `behavior.py::get_reward_zones` logic\n  (scene string parsing + `change_trial=30`).\n- `discretize_reward_distance` — signed distance to the nearest point of the zone and\n  its 7-way discretization.\n- `load_session` / `process_session` — pynwb loading, curation, per-trial assembly.\n- `make_processing_plots` — `--show-processing` diagnostics.\n- `main` — `ProcessPoolExecutor` over sessions, assembly, pickling.\n\nCode inefficiencies identified:\n- Reading the full `(n_frames, n_roi)` `F`/`Fneu` arrays and transposing is the\n  dominant cost (~0.4–1 s per session; the arrays are uncompressed and contiguous).\n- Per-trial Python loops over ~80 trials for the maximin baseline are cheap\n  (~1–3 s/session) because each trial is a vectorised 2-D filter call.\n\nCode speedups added:\n- `iscell` filtering **before** any dF/F maths (halves the working array).\n- Vectorised speed-correlation over all cells at once (single matrix-vector product).\n- `ProcessPoolExecutor` with 8 workers over sessions.\n- `float32` storage for the output arrays.\n\n---\n\n## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\nCommand: `python -u /app/convert_data.py /app/sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing`\nSample = `sub-m11_ses-03` (single-plane, zone switch B→A, the *smallest* session in the\ndataset, 155 `iscell` ROIs) and `sub-m17_ses-05` (**two-plane** animal, zone switch\nC→A in ENV 2) — chosen to exercise both imaging configurations and the switch logic.\n\n### Sample Statistics\n| Statistic | Value Trials / session | 80, 80 |\n| T (frames/trial) | mean 224.1, min 137, max 445 |\n| `time_from_trial_start_s` range | [0.0, 28.6] |\n| `environment` range | [0.0, 1.0] |\n| `trial_number` range | [0.0, 79.0] |\n| `previous_trial_reward` range | [0.0, 1.0] |\n| `reward_zone_distance` distribution | [0.188, 0.082, 0.035, 0.219, 0.024, 0.084, 0.368] |\n| `position` distribution | [0.213, 0.202, 0.213, 0.190, 0.182] |\n| `speed` distribution | [0.053, 0.062, 0.135, 0.505, 0.245] |\n| `lick` distribution | [0.799, 0.201] |\n| `reward_zone_location` distribution | [0.600, 0.168, 0.233] |\n| `reward_outcome` distribution | [0.123, 0.877] |\n\n`verification_sample_out.txt`: **\"Data format is valid, no errors or warnings.\"**\n\nChecks against expectation:\n- Position occupancy is close to uniform over the 5 × 90 cm bins (0.18–0.21), as it\n  must be for an animal that runs the whole track every lap.\n- `reward_zone_location` reflects the two switch sessions: m11 d3 is B(0.42)→A(0.58),\n  m17 d5 is C(0.39)→A(0.61) — i.e. the pre-switch zone occupies 30/80 trials and the\n  post-switch zone 50/80, and there is more *time* per post-switch trial.\n- Reward rate 0.877 / 0.849 per session ≈ the ~15% omission rate.\n- 20% of in-trial frames contain a lick, concentrated in and just before the reward\n  zone (see `processing_sub-m11_ses-03.png`).\n\n### Processing Plots Review\n`processing_sub-m11_ses-03.png` / `processing_sub-m17_ses-05.png` (9 panels, first 6\ntrials) and `*_trials.png` (the final per-trial arrays). No anomalies:\n- Raw `F` shows the laser blanking during the ITI; those samples are NaN in dF/F and\n  are never included in a trial (white gaps in the population raster).\n- Position ramps 0 → 450 cm within every trial; the blue `trial_start` and red\n  `teleport` markers bracket exactly one track traversal.\n- The signed reward-zone distance crosses 0 exactly where the position trace enters\n  the shaded reward zone, and its 7-way discretization steps 0→1→2→3→4→5→6 in order.\n- Speed and its bin index agree at every threshold crossing; the binary lick output\n  is 1 exactly where the lick count is > 0.\n- In the per-trial figure the input `time_from_trial_start_s` starts at 0 for every\n  trial and the per-trial outputs are constant, confirming there is no leakage of one\n  trial's values into the next.\n\n### Run Time Estimates\n| Speed-ups Implemented | Time Savings |\n|---|---|\n| `iscell` filter applied before any dF/F arithmetic | ~2× on the per-session compute and memory |\n| Vectorised speed-correlation (one matrix–vector product instead of a per-cell loop) | ~50× on that step |\n| `ProcessPoolExecutor` over sessions (12 workers) | ~9× wall-clock on the full run |\n| `float32` output arrays | halves pickle size / write time |\n\n| Step | Time / Session | Estimated Total Time |\n|---|---|---|\n| NWB read (`F`, `Fneu`, behaviour) | 0.4 s (m11 d3) – 2.0 s (m4 d14) | ~3 min sequential |\n| dF/F + curation + trial assembly | 0.3 s – 5.9 s | ~7 min sequential |\n| **Measured full run, 12 workers** | — | **46 s** + 14 s pickling = **60 s** |\n\nWell under the 15-minute budget; chance | Ratio | Verdict |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| reward_zone_distance | 0.6200 | 0.1429 | 4.34× | well above chance |\n| position | 0.7657 | 0.2000 | 3.83× | well above chance |\n| speed | 0.6258 | 0.2000 | 3.13× | well above chance |\n| lick | 0.7658 | 0.5000 | 1.53× | above the 1.5× guideline |\n| reward_zone_location | 0.8739 | 0.3333 | 2.62× | well above chance |\n| reward_outcome | 0.6027 | 0.5000 | 1.21× | below 1.5× — investigated below |\n\nNo output is at or below chance.\n\n**`reward_outcome` investigation.** `reward_outcome` is a *per-trial* label, but the\ndecoder predicts it at *every timepoint*. Before the animal reaches the reward zone\nthere is no information anywhere in the brain about whether this trial's reward will\nbe delivered or omitted (omission is decided by a random number generator and is only\nrevealed at the zone), so roughly the first half of every trial is unpredictable by\nconstruction and drags the whole-trial balanced accuracy toward 0.5.\n\nI tested this directly (`cache/diag_reward.py`): for 7 sessions spread across the\ndataset, an independent PCA(100) + balanced logistic regression trained on the first\n75 % of trials and tested on the last 25 % gives\n\n| timepoints used | mean validation balanced accuracy |\n|---|---|\n| all in-trial timepoints | **0.600** |\n| only timepoints past the end of the reward zone | **0.675** |\n\nThe 0.600 from this independent model matches the decoder's 0.6027 almost exactly,\nand restricting to the informative part of the trial raises it by 0.075. So the low\nratio reflects the structure of the task, not a conversion bug. I deliberately did\n**not** \"fix\" it by, e.g., labelling pre-zone timepoints differently — the Decoder\nTask specifies reward outcome as a per-trial output.\n\n**`lick` (1.53×)**: licking is decoded from CA1 dF/F at 0.766 balanced accuracy, i.e.\nthe decoder recovers the lick bouts (clearly visible in `predictions.png`). The\nmodest ratio is simply because a binary variable has chance 0.5.\n\n### Check 2 — Accuracy comparison to the paper\nI searched the paper and `methods.txt` for every reported decoding result. The **only**\ndecoder in the paper is the circular–linear position decoder of Fig. 3\n(`reward_relative/decode.py :: CircularRegression`), and it is reported as a\n**\"decode score\" = mean cos(y − ŷ)** (1 = perfect, 0 = chance) and as a z-score\nrelative to a circular-shift shuffle — not as a classification accuracy, and only for\n*reward-relative position* restricted to RR / TR / non-RR **place-cell subpopulations**\nwithin single sessions.\n\n| Variable | My validation balanced accuracy | Paper's reported accuracy |\n|---|---|---|\n| reward_zone_distance | 0.6200 | no comparable number (Fig. 3 reports a cosine \"decode score\" for a circular RR-position variable, over selected place-cell subsets) |\n| position | 0.7657 | not decoded in the paper |\n| speed | 0.6258 | not decoded in the paper |\n| lick | 0.7658 | not decoded in the paper |\n| reward_zone_location | 0.8739 | not decoded in the paper |\n| reward_outcome | 0.6027 | not decoded in the paper |\n\nThere is therefore no paper number that my accuracies can be lower than. The\nqualitative claim that the paper *does* make — that CA1 population activity carries a\nstrong code for position relative to reward — is reproduced: reward-zone distance is\ndecoded at 4.3× chance and reward-zone identity at 2.6× chance.\n\n### Check 3 — Train vs validation gap\n| Output | Train | Val | Train/Val |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| reward_zone_distance | 0.7901 | 0.6200 | 1.27 |\n| position | 0.8900 | 0.7657 | 1.16 |\n| speed | 0.7226 | 0.6258 | 1.15 |\n| lick | 0.7926 | 0.7658 | 1.03 |\n| reward_zone_location | 0.9639 | 0.8739 | 1.10 |\n| reward_outcome | 0.9379 | 0.6027 | **1.56** |\n\nOnly `reward_outcome` exceeds the 1.5× guideline. Cause: it is constant within a trial\nand the model has 152 session-specific 100 × n_neurons projection matrices trained for\n200 epochs, so it can memorise which *training* trials were rewarded from slow,\ntrial-specific fluctuations in the population state. This is over-fitting of the\nreference decoder on a per-trial label, not data leakage from the conversion:\n- The train/validation split is over **whole trials** (`train_validate_decoder` splits\n  trial indices), so no timepoint of a validation trial is ever seen in training.\n- None of the four decoder **inputs** reveals the current trial's outcome:\n  `previous_trial_reward` is the *previous* trial (verified against the raw NWB for all\n  11,945 consecutive pairs), and `time`, `environment`, `trial_number` are outcome-free.\n- I confirmed with the independent per-session model in Check 1 that ~0.60 is the\n  honest generalisation level for this variable.\n- The same pattern appears for `reward_zone_location` to a much smaller degree\n  (1.10), which is expected because that label is genuinely encoded in the population.\n\n### Other debugging steps run\n1. Output values verified against the raw NWB on specific trials of 4 sessions\n   (Step 10, Check 2) — exact matches.\n2. Temporal alignment verified by plotting neural + inputs + outputs for single trials\n   (`processing_*_trials.png`) and by the whole-dataset boundary statistics\n   (position 0 → 450 cm within each trial, no ITI samples inside trials).\n3. Output variation: no output is degenerate — the most imbalanced is `reward_outcome`\n   at 84.2 % / 15.8 %, and `balanced_loss=True` plus balanced accuracy handle that.\n4. Neural filtering follows the reference (`iscell` + speed-correlation interneuron\n   exclusion), reproducing the paper's per-session minimum of 155 cells and its\n   0.42 ± 0.85 % interneuron exclusion rate (0.35 ± 0.61 % here).\n5. Processing matches the reference `dff()` operation-for-operation (Step 10, Check 3).\n\n### Issues Found and Resolved\nNone remaining. The two real bugs (multi-plane pooling, the 1-frame length mismatch)\nwere found and fixed in Step 10 and everything was re-run afterwards.\n\n---\n\n## Step 13: Documentation and Cleanup\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n- [x] `README.md` created (dataset description, how to load, format spec, key stats)\n- [x] `cache/` folder created with the exploration/validation scripts and\n      `README_CACHE.md`\n- [x] All files organized\n\n### Final deliverables\n| File | Description |\n|---|---|\n| `CONVERSION_NOTES.md` | this document |\n| `convert_data.py` | the conversion script |\n| `converted_data.pkl` | full converted dataset (9.63 GB) |\n| `sample_data.pkl` | 2-session sample (0.08 GB) |\n| `README.md` | user-facing documentation |\n| `conversion_sample_out.txt`, `verification_sample_out.txt`, `train_decoder_sample_out.txt` | sample logs |\n| `conversion_full_out.txt`, `verification_full_out.txt`, `train_decoder_full_out.txt` | full-dataset logs |\n| `processing_sub-m11_ses-03*.png`, `processing_sub-m17_ses-05*.png` | per-step processing diagnostics |\n| `sample_trials.png`, `predictions.png` | decoder sample/prediction plots |\n| `cache/` | exploration and validation scripts |\n","numLines":706,"startLine":1,"totalLines":706}}}

'C'],\n    51→    ['no','yes']\n    52→]\n    53→\n    54→# inspired by Sosa_et_al_2024/src/reward_relative/behavior.py\n    55→reward_zone_dict = {'A': [80, 130],\n    56→                    'B': [200, 250],\n    57→                    'C': [320, 370]}\n    58→\n    59→# =================================================================================================\n    60→# The paper's neural signal processing, COPIED from the paper's code repo\n    61→#\n    62→# The NWB \"Deconvolved\" array is NOT the signal the paper analyses. It is suite2p's own \n    63→# deconvolution of raw fluorescence, and not used in the paper analyses. \n    64→# =================================================================================================\n    65→\n    66→# Experiment days on which the laser was NOT blanked between trials, so the dF/F baseline window is\n    67→# allowed to span the teleport. Animals missing from this table (m3, m4 and m7) were\n    68→# blanked on every day, so their baselines are always restricted to the lap.\n    69→# ** copied from src/reward_relative/teleport_metadata.py::teleport_sessions **\n    70→\n    71→# Dictionary of animals and experiment days for which the teleport periods were imaged\n    72→# (as opposed to blanking the laser)\n    73→\n    74→teleport_sessions = {\n    75→    'GCAMP10': {'exp_days': [1, 7, 8, 14, 15]},\n    76→    'GCAMP11': {'exp_days': [1, 7, 8, 14, 15]},\n    77→    'GCAMP12': {'exp_days': [1, 7, 8, 14, 15]},\n    78→    'GCAMP13': {'exp_days': [1, 7, 8, 14, 15]},\n    79→    'GCAMP14': {'exp_days': [1, 7, 8, 14, 15]},\n    80→    'GCAMP15': {'exp_days': [1, 3, 5, 7, 8, 10, 12, 14, 15, 16, 17]},\n    81→    'GCAMP17': {'exp_days': [1, 3, 5, 7, 8, 10, 12, 14, 15, 16, 17]},\n    82→    'GCAMP18': {'exp_days': [1, 3, 5, 7, 8, 10, 12, 14, 15, 16, 17]},\n    83→    'GCAMP19': {'exp_days': [1, 3, 5, 7, 8, 10, 12, 14, 15, 16, 17]},\n    84→}\n    85→\n    86→\n    87→def nansmooth(a, sig):\n    88→    \"\"\"\n    89→    apply Gaussian smoothing to matrix A containing nans with kernel sig\n    90→    without propagating nans\n    91→    :param a:\n    92→    :param sig:\n    93→    :return:\n    94→\n    95→    ** copied from TwoPUtils/utilities.py::nansmooth **\n    96→    \"\"\"\n    97→\n    98→    # find nans\n    99→    nan_inds = np.isnan(a)\n   100→    a_nanless = np.copy(a)\n   101→    # make nans 0\n   102→    a_nanless[nan_inds] = 0\n   103→\n   104→    # inversely weight nanned indices\n   105→    one = np.ones(a.shape)\n   106→    one[nan_inds] = .001\n   107→    a_nanless = ndimage.gaussian_filter(a_nanless, sig) # was filters.gaussian_filter\n   108→    one = ndimage.gaussian_filter(one, sig) # was filters.gaussian_filter\n   109→    return a_nanless / one\n   110→\n   111→\n   112→def nansmooth_1d(a, sig, axis=-1, return_nans=False, **kwargs):\n   113→    \"\"\"\n   114→    apply Gaussian smoothing to matrix A containing nans with kernel sig\n   115→    without propagating nans\n   116→    :param a:\n   117→    :param sig:\n   118→    :return:\n   119→\n   120→    ** copied from src/reward_relative/utilities.py::nansmooth **\n   121→    (renamed: the TwoPUtils N-D version above already occupies the name `nansmooth`)\n   122→    \"\"\"\n   123→\n   124→    # find nans\n   125→    nan_inds = np.isnan(a)\n   126→    a_nanless = np.copy(a)\n   127→    # make nans 0\n   128→    a_nanless[nan_inds] = 0\n   129→\n   130→    # inversely weight nanned indices\n   131→    one = np.ones(a.shape)\n   132→    one[nan_inds] = 0.001\n   133→    a_nanless = ndimage.gaussian_filter1d(a_nanless, sig, axis=axis, **kwargs) # was filters.gaussian_filter\n   134→    one = ndimage.gaussian_filter1d(one, sig, axis=axis, **kwargs) # was filters.gaussian_filter\n   135→\n   136→    out = a_nanless / one\n   137→    if return_nans:\n   138→        out[nan_inds] = np.nan\n   139→\n   140→    return out\n   141→\n   142→def dff(f,\n   143→        trial_starts,\n   144→        teleports,\n   145→        f_neu=None,\n   146→        neuropil_method=None,\n   147→        baseline_method='maximin',\n   148→        subtract_baseline=True,\n   149→        neu_coef=0.7,\n   150→        tau=0.7,\n   151→        frame_rate=15,\n   152→        n_planes=1,\n   153→        deconvolve=False,\n   154→        keep_teleports=False):\n   155→    \"\"\"\n   156→    Calculate dFF for 1 channel\n   157→\n   158→    :param f: ROI fluorescence timeseries\n   159→    :param trial_starts:\n   160→    :param teleports:\n   161→    :param f_neu: neuropil fluorescence timeseries\n   162→    :param baseline_method:\n   163→    :param subtract_baseline: True gives ∆F/F, False just normalizes F to the basline (divides only)\n   164→    :param neu_coef:\n   165→    :param deconvolve: whether to deconvolve the dff trace into \"events\" (psuedo spikes)\n   166→    :return: dff and spks if deconvolve is True, otherwise just dff\n   167→\n   168→    `trial_starts` and `teleports` must be pandas Series: the keep_teleports branch calls\n   169→    `.to_list()` on `teleports`, which a numpy array does not have. That is their code, unchanged.\n   170→\n   171→    ** copied from src/reward_relative/preprocessing.py::dff **\n   172→    Keeps only the branches this dataset can reach (single-channel)\n   173→    Dropped: bleedthrough_ts, neu_bleedthrough_ts, regress_ts, scrub_ts, neuropil_method='regress',\n   174→    baseline_method='maxsmooth', subtract_baseline=False. \n   175→    \"\"\"\n   176→\n   177→    print('baseline method =', baseline_method)\n   178→\n   179→    # Check frame rate if deconvolving\n   180→    if deconvolve:\n   181→        if (frame_rate/n_planes) > 16:\n   182→            warnings.warn(\n   183→                f\"Using frame rate per plane: {(frame_rate/n_planes)} -- is this correct?\")\n   184→\n   185→    f_ = np.zeros(f.shape) * np.nan\n   186→\n   187→    if f_neu is not None:\n   188→        f_neu_ = np.zeros(f_neu.shape) * np.nan\n   189→\n   190→    if keep_teleports:\n   191→        # if keeping the teleports, keep all the fluorescence from the start of imaging\n   192→        # EXCEPT the teleport sample itself, as this position values might be interpolated between\n   193→        # the end of the track and -50 (where the teleport jitter starts)\n   194→        print(\"keeping teleports\")\n   195→        start_inds = np.append(\n   196→            trial_starts[0],\n   197→            np.array(teleports[:-1] + 2)\n   198→        ).tolist()\n   199→        # ^ add 2 so with start-1 we will start 1 sample after the teleport\n   200→        stop_inds = teleports.to_list()\n   201→        # we will subtract 1 off this index below\n   202→    else:\n   203→        # if not keeping the teleports, keep only the fluorescence on each trial\n   204→        start_inds = trial_starts.tolist()\n   205→        stop_inds = teleports.tolist()\n   206→\n   207→    for i, (start, stop) in enumerate(zip(start_inds, stop_inds)):\n   208→        f_[:, start - 1:stop - 1] = f[:, start - 1:stop - 1]\n   209→\n   210→        if f_neu is not None:\n   211→            f_neu_[:, start - 1:stop - 1] = f_neu[:, start - 1:stop - 1]\n   212→\n   213→    nanmask = ~np.isnan(f_[0, :])\n   214→\n   215→    # once bleedthrough is subtracted, do neuropil correction\n   216→\n   217→    if neuropil_method == 'subtract':\n   218→        f_ -= neu_coef * f_neu_\n   219→\n   220→    print('No minimum subtraction')\n   221→\n   222→    # Caluclate baseline for chan 1\n   223→    flow = np.zeros(f_.shape) * np.nan\n   224→    spks = np.zeros(f_.shape) * np.nan\n   225→\n   226→    # get baseline within each trial\n   227→    for i, (start, stop) in enumerate(zip(start_inds, stop_inds)):\n   228→\n   229→        # if we subtracted neuropil, add back in the neuropil mean in each trial\n   230→        # such that dff values will be close to true ∆F/F, and we don't divide by small numbers\n   231→        if neuropil_method == 'subtract':\n   232→            f_[:, start-1:stop-1] = f_[:, start-1:stop-1] + neu_coef * np.nanmean(\n   233→                f_neu_[:, start-1:stop-1], axis=1, keepdims=True)\n   234→\n   235→        if baseline_method == 'maximin':\n   236→            # cut out ITIs and smooth signal\n   237→            flow[:, start - 1:stop -\n   238→                 1] = nansmooth(f_[:, start-1:stop-1], [0, 15])\n   239→\n   240→            # minimum filter, taking min val over 20 sec\n   241→            flow[:, start-1:stop-1] = ndimage.minimum_filter1d(\n   242→                flow[:, start-1:stop-1], int(300), axis=-1) # was sp.ndimage.filters.minimum_filter1d\n   243→            flow[:, start-1:stop-1] = ndimage.maximum_filter1d(\n   244→                flow[:, start-1:stop-1], int(300), axis=-1)  # max filter with same window (dilation), was sp.ndimage.filters.maximum_filter1d\n   245→\n   246→    # to get deltaF/F: subtract baseline from initial signal, divide by abs(baseline)\n   247→    # baseline can some times end up as negative due to regression\n   248→    dff = np.zeros(f_.shape) * np.nan\n   249→    if subtract_baseline:\n   250→        dff[:, nanmask] = (f_[:, nanmask] - flow[:, nanmask]\n   251→                           ) / np.abs(flow[:, nanmask])\n   252→\n   253→    print('dff:', dff.shape, 'min ', np.nanmin(dff), 'max ', np.nanmax(dff))\n   254→\n   255→    # Smooth the deltaF/F transients by 2 time bins\n   256→    for i, (start, stop) in enumerate(zip(start_inds, stop_inds)):\n   257→        dff[:, start - 1:stop -\n   258→            1] = nansmooth_1d(dff[:, start - 1:stop - 1], 2, axis=1)\n   259→        # After smoothing: Get \"spikes\" with calcium kernel deconvolution from suite2p\n   260→        if deconvolve:\n   261→            spks[:, start-1:stop-1] = dcnv.oasis(\n   262→                np.ascontiguousarray(dff[:, start-1:stop-1], dtype=np.float32),\n   263→                2000, tau, (frame_rate/n_planes)) # this is float64 in the original code\n   264→\n   265→    if deconvolve:\n   266→        return dff, spks\n   267→    else:\n   268→        return dff\n   269→\n   270→\n   271→def is_putative_interneuron(dff, speed, r_thresh=0.5):\n   272→    \"\"\"\n   273→    Find putative interneurons based on correlation of dF/F with speed\n   274→    (under the hypothesis that fast-spiking interneurons in the pyramidal\n   275→    layer are often highly correlated with running speed).\n   276→\n   277→    :param dff: (ncells, T) dF/F timeseries, NaN outside the analysed windows\n   278→    :param speed: (T,) running speed in cm/s\n   279→    :param r_thresh: threshold for speed correlation r; dayData sets int_thresh = 0.5, which is\n   280→        the value the Methods quote (\"a Pearson correlation of >0.5\"). Note that the threshold\n   281→        defaulted to 0.3 in the source function; the analyses always pass 0.5.\n   282→    :return: is_int (array) with a Boolean for each ROI, where putative interneuron is True.\n   283→\n   284→    ** copied from src/reward_relative/spatial.py::is_putative_interneuron **\n   285→    Change: takes the dF/F and speed arrays as arguments instead of reading them off a TwoPUtils \n   286→    `sess` object, which this conversion does not build.\n   287→    \"\"\"\n   288→    speed_corr = np.zeros((dff.shape[0],))\n   289→    nanmask = ~np.isnan(dff[0, :])\n   290→\n   291→    for c in range(dff.shape[0]):\n   292→        speed_corr[c] = np.corrcoef(\n   293→            dff[c, nanmask], speed[nanmask])[0, 1]\n   294→\n   295→    is_int = speed_corr > r_thresh\n   296→\n   297→    return is_int\n   298→\n   299→# end copy-paste\n   300→# =================================================================================================\n   301→\n   302→\n   303→def get_subjects(datadir=DATADIR):\n   304→    # find all directories in datadir that start with 'sub-'\n   305→    subjects = [d.split('-')[1] for d in os.listdir(datadir) if d.startswith('sub-')]\n   306→    return subjects\n   307→\n   308→def get_sessions(subject, datadir=DATADIR):\n   309→    subject_dir = os.path.join(datadir, f\"sub-{subject}\")\n   310→    # parse sessions from files named sub-<subject>-<session>_behavior+ophys.nwb\n   311→    nwb_files = [f for f in os.listdir(subject_dir) if f.endswith('.nwb')]\n   312→    sessions = [int(f.split('_')[1].split('-')[1]) for f in nwb_files]\n   313→    return sessions\n   314→\n   315→def get_nwb_file(subject, session, datadir=DATADIR):\n   316→    nwb_file_path = os.path.join(datadir, f\"sub-{subject}\", f\"sub-{subject}_ses-{session:02d}_behavior+ophys.nwb\")\n   317→    assert os.path.exists(nwb_file_path), f\"File {nwb_file_path} does not exist\"\n   318→    return nwb_file_path\n   319→\n   320→def interp(new_idx,old_idx,old_data,**kwargs):\n   321→    f = interp1d(old_idx, old_data, axis=0, **kwargs)\n   322→    return f(new_idx)\n   323→\n   324→\n   325→def get_trial_starts_ends(trial_start,teleport):\n   326→    trial_start_idx = np.nonzero(trial_start)[0]\n   327→    is_teleport_start = (teleport[1:] > 0) & (teleport[:-1] <= 0)\n   328→    trial_end_idx = np.nonzero(is_teleport_start)[0] + 1 \n   329→    assert len(trial_start_idx) == len(trial_end_idx), f\"Number of trial starts ({len(trial_start_idx)}) does not match number of trial ends ({len(trial_end_idx)})\"\n   330→    return trial_start_idx, trial_end_idx\n   331→        \n   332→\n   333→def survey_session_data(subject, session, datadir=DATADIR):\n   334→    nwb_file_path = get_nwb_file(subject, session, datadir)\n   335→    info = {}\n   336→    info['reward_zone_positions'] = []\n   337→    with pynwb.NWBHDF5IO(nwb_file_path, 'r') as io:\n   338→        nwbfile = io.read()\n   339→        # parse neural activity data\n   340→        deconvolved_data = nwbfile.processing[\"ophys\"][\"Deconvolved\"]\n   341→        spike_data = []\n   342→        nplanes = len(deconvolved_data.roi_response_series)\n   343→        for plane_data in deconvolved_data.roi_response_series.values():\n   344→            roi_data = plane_data.rois[:]\n   345→            iscell = np.array(roi_data['iscell'].to_list())[:,0] == 1\n   346→            spike_data_curr = plane_data.data[:]\n   347→            assert spike_data_curr.shape[1] == iscell.shape[0], f\"Number of neurons in data ({spike_data_curr.shape[1]}) does not match number of iscell entries ({iscell.shape[0]})\"\n   348→            spike_data.append(spike_data_curr[:,iscell])\n   349→        spike_data = np.concatenate(spike_data, axis=1)\n   350→        info['T'], info['nneurons'] = spike_data.shape\n   351→        max_spike_rate = np.max(spike_data)\n   352→        info['max_spike_rate_range'] = (max_spike_rate.min(), max_spike_rate.max())\n   353→        info['neural_time_bin_size'] = nplanes/plane_data.rate*1000 # ms\n   354→\n   355→        behavior_data = nwbfile.processing['behavior']['BehavioralTimeSeries'].time_series       \n   356→        istrial = behavior_data['trial number'].data[:] >= 0\n   357→        # reward_amounts are all the same\n   358→        reward_amounts = behavior_data['Reward'].data[:]\n   359→        info['unique_reward_amounts'] = np.unique(reward_amounts)\n   360→        info['nrewards'] = len(reward_amounts)\n   361→        # autoreward is all 0s, so not useful for modeling, but check that it is all zeros\n   362→        autoreward = behavior_data['autoreward'].data[:]\n   363→        info['unique_autoreward'] = np.unique(autoreward[istrial])\n   364→        # vr environment\n   365→        vr_environment = behavior_data['environment'].data[:] # one value per time point, does this change by session?\n   366→        info['unique_vr_environment'] = np.unique(vr_environment[istrial])\n   367→        licks = behavior_data['lick'].data[:]\n   368→        info['unique_licks'] = np.unique(licks[istrial])\n   369→        position = behavior_data['position'].data[:]\n   370→        info['position_range'] = (position[istrial].min(), position[istrial].max())\n   371→        reward_zone = behavior_data['reward_zone'].data[:]\n   372→        info['unique_reward_zone'] = np.unique(reward_zone[istrial])\n   373→        scanning = behavior_data['scanning'].data[:]\n   374→        info['unique_scanning'] = np.unique(scanning[istrial])\n   375→        speed = behavior_data['speed'].data[:]\n   376→        info['speed_range'] = (speed[istrial].min(), speed[istrial].max())\n   377→        teleport = behavior_data['teleport'].data[:]\n   378→        info['unique_teleport'] = np.unique(teleport[istrial])\n   379→        \n   380→        # trial number and trial start do not agree with each other\n   381→        # use trial_start and teleport to find starts and ends of trials\n   382→        trial_start_idx, trial_end_idx = get_trial_starts_ends(behavior_data['trial_start'].data[:], teleport)\n   383→        trial_length = trial_end_idx - trial_start_idx\n   384→        info['trial_length_range'] = (trial_length.min(), trial_length.max())\n   385→\n   386→        ntrials = len(trial_start_idx)\n   387→        info['ntrials'] = ntrials\n   388→        info['reward_zone_positions'] = np.full((ntrials, 2), np.nan)\n   389→        for trial in range(ntrials):\n   390→            idx = np.arange(trial_start_idx[trial], trial_end_idx[trial], dtype=int)\n   391→            pos_trial = position[idx]\n   392→            reward_zone_trial = reward_zone[idx] > 0\n   393→            if not np.any(reward_zone_trial):\n   394→                continue\n   395→            info['reward_zone_positions'][trial] = [np.min(pos_trial[reward_zone_trial]), np.max(pos_trial[reward_zone_trial])]\n   396→                    \n   397→    return info\n   398→    \n   399→def get_bin_centers(bin_edges,data):\n   400→    # replace infinity with the max/min of the data for plotting purposes\n   401→    bin_edges_plot = np.array(bin_edges.copy())\n   402→    if np.isinf(bin_edges_plot[0]):\n   403→        bin_edges_plot[0] = data.min()\n   404→        if bin_edges_plot[0] > bin_edges_plot[1]:\n   405→            bin_edges_plot[0] = 2*bin_edges_plot[1] - bin_edges_plot[2] # extrapolate to get a lower value for the first bin edge\n   406→    if np.isinf(bin_edges_plot[-1]):\n   407→        bin_edges_plot[-1] = data.max()\n   408→        if bin_edges_plot[-1] < bin_edges_plot[-2]:\n   409→            bin_edges_plot[-1] = 2*bin_edges_plot[-2] - bin_edges_plot[-3] # extrapolate to get a higher value for the last bin edge\n   410→    bin_centers = (bin_edges_plot[:-1] + bin_edges_plot[1:]) / 2\n   411→    return bin_centers\n   412→    \n   413→def compute_distance_to_reward_zone(position_curr, reward_zone_curr):\n   414→    ntimepoints = len(position_curr)\n   415→    reward_zone_range = reward_zone_dict[reward_zone_curr]\n   416→    distance_to_reward_zone = np.zeros(ntimepoints)\n   417→    idx_before_zone = position_curr < reward_zone_range[0]\n   418→    distance_to_reward_zone[idx_before_zone] = position_curr[idx_before_zone] - reward_zone_range[0] # negative if before the zone\n   419→    idx_after_zone = position_curr > reward_zone_range[1]\n   420→    distance_to_reward_zone[idx_after_zone] = position_curr[idx_after_zone] - reward_zone_range[1] # positive if after the zone\n   421→    return distance_to_reward_zone\n   422→    \n   423→def convert_session_data(subject, session, info, trial_offset, time_bin_size=None, datadir=DATADIR, \n   424→                         show_processing=False, max_neurons_plot=30, min_ntimepoints=50,\n   425→                         figfile=None):\n   426→\n   427→    nwb_file_path = get_nwb_file(subject, session, datadir)\n   428→    io = pynwb.NWBHDF5IO(nwb_file_path, 'r')\n   429→    nwbfile = io.read()\n   430→\n   431→    # parse neural activity data: the raw suite2p traces the paper computes its dF/F and\n   432→    # deconvolved events from. The deconvolution needs the trial boundaries, so it runs further\n   433→    # below, once those have been parsed out of the behavior data.\n   434→    fluorescence_data = nwbfile.processing[\"ophys\"][\"Fluorescence\"]\n   435→    neuropil_data = nwbfile.processing[\"ophys\"][\"Neuropil\"]\n   436→    fluorescence = []\n   437→    neuropil = []\n   438→    nplanes = len(fluorescence_data.roi_response_series)\n   439→    for plane_name, plane_data in fluorescence_data.roi_response_series.items():\n   440→        roi_data = plane_data.rois[:]\n   441→        iscell = np.array(roi_data['iscell'].to_list())[:,0] == 1\n   442→        fluorescence_curr = plane_data.data[:]\n   443→        neuropil_curr = neuropil_data.roi_response_series[plane_name].data[:]\n   444→        assert fluorescence_curr.shape[1] == iscell.shape[0], f\"Number of neurons in data ({fluorescence_curr.shape[1]}) does not match number of iscell entries ({iscell.shape[0]})\"\n   445→        assert neuropil_curr.shape == fluorescence_curr.shape, f\"Neuropil shape ({neuropil_curr.shape}) does not match fluorescence shape ({fluorescence_curr.shape})\"\n   446→        # Subset to the curated cells now rather than after dff. Every step of dff is per-cell --\n   447→        # its only cross-cell operation is a Gaussian with sigma 0 on the cell axis -- so this is\n   448→        # numerically identical to subsetting afterwards, on a third of the memory.\n   449→        fluorescence.append(fluorescence_curr[:,iscell])\n   450→        neuropil.append(neuropil_curr[:,iscell])\n   451→    T = fluorescence[0].shape[0]\n   452→    # The rate stored on the series is the scanner rate: with two planes interleaved it is twice\n   453→    # the rate at which any one plane is sampled, which is why dff takes n_planes as well.\n   454→    frame_rate = plane_data.rate\n   455→    neural_time_bin_size = nplanes/frame_rate*1000 # ms\n   456→\n   457→    behavior_data = nwbfile.processing['behavior']['BehavioralTimeSeries'].time_series\n   458→    \n   459→    timestamps = behavior_data['trial number'].timestamps[:] \n   460→    if T != len(timestamps):\n   461→        print(f\"Warning: number of neural time bins ({T}) does not match number of behavior timestamps ({len(timestamps)}), cropping to the minimum of the two\")\n   462→        T = min(T, len(timestamps))\n   463→        fluorescence = [f[:T] for f in fluorescence]\n   464→        neuropil = [f[:T] for f in neuropil]\n   465→        timestamps = timestamps[:T]\n   466→    \n   467→    behavior_time_bin_size = np.median(np.diff(timestamps))*1000 # ms\n   468→    \n   469→    # trial number for each time point\n   470→    trial_start_idx, trial_end_idx = get_trial_starts_ends(behavior_data['trial_start'].data[:], behavior_data['teleport'].data[:])\n   471→    ntrials = len(trial_start_idx)\n   472→    \n   473→    # reward_amounts are all the same\n   474→    reward_amounts = behavior_data['Reward'].data[:][:T]\n   475→    assert np.allclose(reward_amounts,reward_amounts[0]), f\"Reward amounts are not all the same, unique values: {np.unique(reward_amounts)}\"\n   476→    reward_times = behavior_data['Reward'].timestamps[:] # in seconds        \n   477→    # find the closest timestamp to reward times, not careful for mismatches right now\n   478→    reward_time_indices = np.searchsorted(timestamps, reward_times)\n   479→    err = timestamps[reward_time_indices] - reward_times\n   480→    assert np.all(np.abs(err) <= time_bin_size/2), f\"Reward times do not align with neural time bins, max error: {np.max(np.abs(err))} s\"\n   481→    isreward = np.zeros(T)\n   482→    isreward[reward_time_indices] = 1\n   483→\n   484→    # autoreward is all 0s, so not useful for modeling, but check that it is all zeros\n   485→\n   486→    # vr environment\n   487→    vr_environment = behavior_data['environment'].data[:][:T] # one value per time point, does this change by session?\n   488→    assert np.allclose(behavior_data['environment'].timestamps[:][:T], timestamps), \"Timestamps for environment do not match trial number timestamps\"\n   489→\n   490→    # 14, and mice m15-m19 on day 1 and all switch days\" -- and names m17 and m18 as the two\n   524→    # animals imaged in two planes, which are the two-plane sessions in this dataset. Session\n   525→    # number is the experiment day: m11 starts at ses-03, and the paper says imaging for m11\n   526→    # started on day 3.\n   527→    keep_teleports = session in teleport_sessions.get(f'GCAMP{subject[1:]}', {}).get('exp_days', [])\n   528→    spike_data = []\n   529→    nint = 0\n   530→    for fluorescence_curr, neuropil_curr in zip(fluorescence, neuropil):\n   531→        # The trial indices are passed in shifted by +1: dff slices its windows as\n   532→        # [start-1, stop-1), one sample earlier than the [trial_start, teleport) laps emitted here\n   533→        # -- which is the window their own behavior.py:57 takes from the same two index arrays, so\n   534→        # the two files already disagree by a sample upstream. The one cost is that on\n   537→        # keep_teleports sessions the baseline window opens two samples after the teleport onset\n   538→        # rather than their one.\n   539→        dff_curr,        f_neu=neuropil_curr.T.astype(np.float64),\n   542→                                    neuropil_method='subtract', baseline_method='maximin',\n   543→                                    subtract_baseline=True, neu_coef=0.7, tau=0.7,\n   544→                                    frame_rate=frame_rate, n_planes=nplanes, deconvolve=True,\n   545→                                    keep_teleports=keep_teleports)\n   546→        # Drop the putative interneurons the paper excludes from further analysis.\n   547→        isint = is_putative_interneuron(dff_curr, speed)\n   548→        nint += int(np.sum(isint))\n   549→        spike_data.append(events_curr[~isint].T)\n   550→    spike_data = np.concatenate(spike_data, axis=1)\n   551→    print(f\"Excluded {nint} putative interneurons of {nint + spike_data.shape[1]} cells \"\n   552→          f\"({100*nint/(nint + spike_data.shape[1]):.2f}%)\")\n   553→    # Samples between laps are NaN by construction (that is what 566→    istrial = np.ones(ntrials,dtype=bool) # keep track of whether we have dropped a trial for having too few time points\n   567→    min_distance_to_reward_zone = np.inf\n   568→    max_distance_to_reward_zone = -np.inf\n   569→    for trial in range(ntrials):\n   570→        idx = np.arange(trial_start_idx[trial], trial_end_idx[trial], dtype=int)\n   571→        if idx.sum() <  607→        \n   608→        output_curr = np.full((noutputs, ntimepoints), -1)\n   609→\n   610→        # distance to reward zone\n   611→        reward_zone_curr = reward_zone[trial].item()\n   612→        distance_to_reward_zone = compute_distance_to_reward_zone(position_curr, reward_zone_curr)\n   613→        min_distance_to_reward_zone = min(distance_to_reward_zone.min(), min_distance_to_reward_zone)\n   614→        max_distance_to_reward_zone = max(distance_to_reward_zone.max(), max_distance_to_reward_zone)\n   615→\n   616→        # histogram distance to reward zone     output_curr[1] = np.digitize(position_curr, position_bins) - 1\n   621→        \n   622→        # speed\n   623→        output_curr[2] = np.digitize(speed_curr, speed_bins) - 1\n   624→        \n   625→        # lick\n   626→        output_curr[3] = (licks_curr > 0).astype(int)\n   627→        \n   628→        # reward location\n   629→        output_curr[4] = ['A','B','C'].index(reward_zone_curr)\n   630→        \n   631→        # reward outcome\n   632→        output_curr[5] = np.any(isreward[idx]).astype(int)\n   633→        \n   634→        output.append(output_curr)\n   635→        \n   636→    if show_processing:\n   637→        \n   638→        ntrials = len(neural)\n   639→        trials_plot = np.linspace(0, ntrials-1, min(ntrials, 5), dtype=int)\n   640→        nbehplot = 5\n   641→        if not np.isinf(max_neurons_plot):\n   642→            sharey = 'row'\n   643→        else:\n   644→            sharey = False\n   645→        fig, ax = plt.subplots(nbehplot+1, 5, figsize=(20, 20),\n   646→                                gridspec_kw={'height_ratios': [nbehplot,] + [1]*nbehplot},\n   647→                                sharex='col', sharey=sharey)\n   648→        \n   649→        distance_bin_centers = get_bin_centers(distance_to_reward_zone_bins, np.array([min_distance_to_reward_zone, max_distance_to_reward_zone]))\n   650→        position_bin_centers = get_bin_centers(position_bins, position[trial_start_idx[0]:trial_end_idx[-1]])\n   651→        speed_bin_centers = get_bin_centers(speed_bins, speed[trial_start_idx[0]:trial_end_idx[-1]])\n   652→        \n   653→        for triali, trial in enumerate(trials_plot):\n   654→            input_curr = input[trial]\n   655→            output_curr = output[trial]\n   656→            neural_curr = neural[trial]\n   657→            nneurons = np.minimum(max_neurons_plot,neural_curr.shape[0])\n   658→            idx = offset for visibility\n   663→\n   664→            ax[1,triali].plot(timestamps_curr,input_curr[1],'.-') # vr environment\n   665→            \n   666→            reward_zone_curr = reward_zone[trial].item()\n   667→            position_curr = position[idx]\n   668→            speed_curr = speed[idx]\n   669→            licks_curr = licks[idx]\n   670→            distance_to_reward_zone = compute_distance_to_reward_zone(position_curr, reward_zone_curr)\n   671→            ax[2,triali].plot(timestamps_curr,distance_to_reward_zone,'.-',label='raw') # distance to reward zone\n   672→            ax[2,triali].plot(timestamps_curr,distance_bin_centers[output_curr[0]],'-',label='thresholded') # thresholded distance to reward zone\n   673→            ax[3,triali].plot(timestamps_curr,position_curr,'.-',label='raw') # position\n   674→            ax[3,triali].plot(timestamps_curr,position_bin_centers[output_curr[1]],'-',label='thresholded') # thresholded position\n   675→            ax[4,triali].plot(timestamps_curr,speed_curr,'.-',label='raw') # speed\n   676→            ax[4,triali].plot(timestamps_curr,speed_bin_centers[output_curr[2]],'-',label='thresholded') # thresholded speed\n   677→            ax[5,triali].plot(timestamps_curr,licks_curr,'.-',label='raw') # lick\n   678→            ax[5,triali].plot(timestamps_curr,output_curr[3],'-',label='thresholded') # thresholded lick\n   679→        \n   680→        if not np.isinf(max_neurons_plot):\n   681→            ax[0,0].set_ylim(-1, max_neurons_plot+1)\n   682→        ax[0,0].set_ylabel('Neural activity')\n   683→        ax[1,0].set_ylabel('VR environment')\n   684→        ax[2,0].set_ylabel('Distance to reward zone (cm)')\n   685→        ax[2,0].legend()\n   686→        ax[3,0].set_ylabel('Position (cm)')\n   687→        ax[4,0].set_ylabel('Speed (cm/s)')\n   688→        ax[5,0].set_ylabel('Lick')\n   689→        \n   690→        plt.tight_layout()\n   691→        plt.show()\n   692→        if figfile is not None:\n   693→            fig.savefig(figfile)\n   694→        \n   695→    return neural, input, output\n   696→\n   697→def survey(force=False, datadir=DATADIR, surveyfile=SURVEYFILE):\n   698→    if not force and os.path.exists(surveyfile):\n   699→        print(f\"Loading existing survey from {surveyfile}\")\n   700→        with open(surveyfile, 'rb') as f:\n   701→            return pickle.load(f)\n   702→    all_info = {}\n   703→    subjects = get_subjects(datadir=datadir)\n   704→    all_info['reward_zone_positions'] = {}\n   705→    fig,ax = plt.subplots(1,len(subjects), figsize=(2*len(subjects),20),sharex=True,sharey=True)\n   706→\n   707→    for i,subject in enumerate(subjects):\n   708→        sessions = get_sessions(subject, datadir=datadir)\n   709→        print(f\"Subject: {subject}, Sessions: {sessions}\")\n   710→        all_info['reward_zone_positions'][subject] = np.zeros((0,2))\n   711→        for session in sessions:\n   712→            info = survey_session_data(subject, session, datadir=datadir)\n   713→            print(f\"Subject {subject}, Session {session}:\")\n   714→            for key, value in info.items():\n   715→                print(f\"  {key}: {value}\")\n   716→            for key,value in info.items():\n   717→                if key == 'reward_zone_positions':\n   718→                    all_info['reward_zone_positions'][subject] = np.concatenate((all_info['reward_zone_positions'][subject], value), axis=0)\n   719→                elif key == 'neural_time_bin_size':\n   720→                    if 'neural_time_bin_size' not in all_info:\n   721→                        all_info['neural_time_bin_size'] = [value,]\n   722→                    else:\n   723→                        all_info['neural_time_bin_size'].append(value)\n   724→                elif not ('range' in key or 'unique' in key):\n   725→                    if 'range_'+key not in all_info:\n   726→                        all_info['range_'+key] = (value, value)\n   727→                    else:\n   728→                        all_info['range_'+key] = (min(all_info.get('range_'+key, (value, value))[0], value), max(all_info.get('range_'+key, (value, value))[1], value))\n   729→                elif key not in all_info:\n   730→                    all_info[key] = value\n   731→                elif 'range' in key:\n   732→                    all_info[key] = (min(all_info[key][0], value[0]), max(all_info[key][1], value[1]))\n   733→                elif 'unique' in key:\n   734→                    all_info[key] = np.unique(np.concatenate((all_info[key], value)))\n   735→                else:\n   736→                    raise ValueError(f\"Unexpected key in info: {key}\")\n   737→        # plot a horizontal line from reward_zone_positions[0] to reward_zone_positions[1] for each trial, with y value equal to the trial number\n   738→        reward_zone_positions = all_info['reward_zone_positions'][subject]\n   739→        for trial in range(reward_zone_positions.shape[0]):\n   740→            ax[i].plot(reward_zone_positions[trial], [trial, trial], '-')\n   741→        ax[i].set_title(f\"Subject {subject}\")\n   742→\n   743→                    \n   744→    print(\"Summary across all sessions:\")\n   745→    for key, value in all_info.items():\n   746→        if key == 'reward_zone_positions':\n   747→            continue\n   748→        elif 'range' in key:\n   749→            print(f\"  {key}: {value[0]} to {value[1]}\")\n   750→        elif 'unique' in key:\n   751→            print(f\"  {key}: {value}\")\n   752→        else:\n   753→            print(f\"  {key}: {value}\")\n   754→            \n   755→    ax[0].set_xlabel('Position (cm)')\n   756→    ax[0].set_ylabel('Trial')\n   757→    fig.tight_layout()\n   758→    fig.savefig('reward_zone_positions.png')\n   759→    fig.savefig('reward_zone_positions.pdf')\n   760→    fig.savefig('reward_zone_positions.svg')\n   761→    \n   762→    segment_results = segment_reward_positions(all_info['reward_zone_positions'])\n   763→    all_info['reward_zone_labels'] = {k: v['zone_labels'] for k,v in segment_results.items()}\n   764→    all_info['reward_zone_states'] = {k: v['states'] for k,v in segment_results.items()}\n   765→    all_info['reward_zone_dist'] = {k: v['zone_dist'] for k,v in segment_results.items()}\n   766→    \n   767→    with open(surveyfile, 'wb') as f:\n   768→        pickle.dump(all_info, f)\n   769→        \n   770→    return all_info\n   771→\n   772→def segment_reward_positions(reward_zone_positions):\n   773→    # assign each trial to one of the reward zones defined in reward_zone_dict\n   774→    # use viterbi algorithm with a high cost for switching to encourage stable assignments\n   775→    zone_names = sorted(reward_zone_dict.keys())\n   776→    n_states = len(zone_names)\n   777→    zone_ranges = np.array([reward_zone_dict[z] for z in zone_names])  # (n_states, 2)\n   778→    pstay = .9\n   779→    sigma = 10.0  # std dev for Gaussian emission on zone distance\n   780→\n   781→    # transition log-probabilities\n   782→    log_trans = np.full((n_states, n_states), np.log((1 - pstay) / (n_states - 1)))\n   783→    np.fill_diagonal(log_trans, np.log(pstay))\n   784→\n   785→    results = {}\n   786→\n   787→    for mouse_id, positions in reward_zone_positions.items():\n   788→        n_trials = positions.shape[0]\n   789→        startpoints = positions[:,0] # use the start point as it looks cleaner\n   790→        valid = ~np.isnan(startpoints)\n   791→\n   792→        # emission log-probabilities: Gaussian penalty based on distance to zone range\n   793→        # distance is 0 if inside [lo, hi], otherwise distance to nearest edge\n   794→        # for NaN trials, uniform emission (all zeros) so assignment is driven by transitions\n   795→        log_emission = np.zeros((n_trials, n_states))\n   796→        dist = np.maximum(zone_ranges[None, :, 0] - startpoints[valid, None],\n   797→                          startpoints[valid, None] - zone_ranges[None, :, 1]) # negative if inside the zone, positive if outside\n   798→        dist = np.maximum(dist, 0)\n   799→        log_emission[valid] = -0.5 * (dist / sigma) ** 2\n   800→\n   801→        # viterbi forward pass\n   802→        log_delta = np.zeros((n_trials, n_states))\n   803→        psi = np.zeros((n_trials, n_states), dtype=int)\n   804→        log_delta[0] = log_emission[0]  # uniform prior\n   805→        for t in range(1, n_trials):\n   806→            for k in range(n_states):\n   807→                scores = log_delta[t - 1] + log_trans[:, k]\n   808→                psi[t, k] = np.argmax(scores)\n   809→                log_delta[t, k] = scores[psi[t, k]] + log_emission[t, k]\n   810→\n   811→        # backtrack\n   812→        states = np.zeros(n_trials, dtype=int)\n   813→        states[-1] = np.argmax(log_delta[-1])\n   814→        for t in range(n_trials - 2, -1, -1):\n   815→            states[t] = psi[t + 1, states[t + 1]]\n   816→\n   817→        # convert state indices to zone names\n   818→        zone_labels = np.array([zone_names[s] for s in states])\n   819→\n   820→        # distance from each trial's startpoint to its assigned zone range\n   821→        assigned_lo = zone_ranges[states, 0]\n   822→        assigned_hi = zone_ranges[states, 1]\n   823→        zone_dist = np.maximum(np.maximum(assigned_lo - startpoints, startpoints - assigned_hi), 0)\n   824→        zone_dist[~valid] = np.nan\n   825→\n   826→        results[mouse_id] = {'zone_labels': zone_labels, 'states': states, 'zone_dist': zone_dist}\n   827→\n   828→    return results\n   829→    \n   830→\n   831→def main():\n   832→\n   833→    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description='Convert Sosa et al. 2024 NWB data to pickle format.')\n   834→    parser.add_argument('outpicklefile', type=str, nargs='?', default='converted_data.pkl', help='Output pickle file path')\n   835→    parser.add_argument('--datadir', type=str, default=DATADIR, help='Directory containing NWB data files')\n   836→    parser.add_argument('--surveyfile', type=str, default=SURVEYFILE, help='Path to survey info pickle file (for saving/loading)')\n   837→    parser.add_argument('--show-processing', action='store_true', help='Plot visualizations of processing steps for up to 2 sessions')\n   838→    mode_group = parser.add_mutually_exclusive_group()\n   839→    mode_group.add_argument('--sample', action='store_true', help='Process only 2 sessions for testing')\n   840→    mode_group.add_argument('--full', action='store_true', default=True, help='Process all sessions (default)')\n   841→    mode_group.add_argument('--survey-only', action='store_true', help='Only run survey step (generate survey_info.pkl), no conversion')\n   842→    args = parser.parse_args()\n   843→\n   844→    # --sample implies showing at most 2 sessions\n   845→    max_sessions = 2 if args.sample else None\n   846→    show_processing = args.show_processing\n find nans\n    nan_inds = np.isnan(a)\n    a_nanless = np.copy(a)\n    # make nans 0\n    a_nanless[nan_inds] = 0\n\n    # inversely weight nanned indices\n    one = np.ones(a.shape)\n    one[nan_inds] = .001\n    a_nanless = ndimage.gaussian_filter(a_nanless, sig) # was filters.gaussian_filter\n    one = ndimage.gaussian_filter(one, sig) # was filters.gaussian_filter\n    return a_nanless / one\n\n\ndef nansmooth_1d(a, sig, axis=-1, return_nans=False, **kwargs):\n    \"\"\"\n    apply Gaussian smoothing to matrix A containing nans with kernel sig\n    without propagating nans\n    :param a:\n    :param sig:\n    :return:\n\n    ** copied from src/reward_relative/utilities.py::nansmooth **\n    (renamed: the TwoPUtils N-D version above already occupies the name `nansmooth`)\n    \"\"\"\n\n    # find nans\n    nan_inds = np.isnan(a)\n    a_nanless = np.copy(a)\n    # make nans 0\n    a_nanless[nan_inds] = 0\n\n    # inversely weight nanned indices\n    one = np.ones(a.shape)\n    one[nan_inds] = 0.001\n    a_nanless = ndimage.gaussian_filter1d(a_nanless, sig, axis=axis, **kwargs) # was filters.gaussian_filter\n    one = ndimage.gaussian_filter1d(one, sig, axis=axis, **kwargs) # was filters.gaussian_filter\n\n    out = a_nanless / one\n    if return_nans:\n        out[nan_inds] = np.nan\n\n    return out\n\ndef dff(f,\n        trial_starts,\n        teleports,\n        f_neu=None,\n        neuropil_method=None,\n        baseline_method='maximin',\n        subtract_baseline=True,\n        neu_coef=0.7,\n        tau=0.7,\n        frame_rate=15,\n        n_planes=1,\n        deconvolve=False,\n        keep_teleports=False):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate dFF for 1 channel\n\n    :param f: ROI fluorescence timeseries\n    :param trial_starts:\n    :param teleports:\n    :param f_neu: neuropil fluorescence timeseries\n    :param baseline_method:\n    :param subtract_baseline: True gives ∆F/F, False just normalizes F to the basline (divides only)\n    :param neu_coef:\n    :param deconvolve: whether to deconvolve the dff trace into \"events\" (psuedo spikes)\n    :return: dff and spks if deconvolve is True, otherwise just dff\n\n    `trial_starts` and `teleports` must be pandas Series: the keep_teleports branch calls\n    `.to_list()` on `teleports`, which a numpy array does not have.
